Unicode__UCD.3perl

Paquete: perl

Licencias: Artistic-1.0-Perl OR GPL-1.0-or-later

Unicode::UCD(3perl) Guía de referencia del programador de Perl Unicode::UCD(3perl)

NOMBRE

Unicode::UCD - Base de datos de caracteres Unicode

SINOPSIS

use Unicode::UCD 'charinfo';
my $charinfo   = charinfo($codepoint);
use Unicode::UCD 'charprop';
my $value  = charprop($codepoint, $property);
use Unicode::UCD 'charprops_all';
my $all_values_hash_ref = charprops_all($codepoint);
use Unicode::UCD 'casefold';
my $casefold = casefold($codepoint);
use Unicode::UCD 'all_casefolds';
my $all_casefolds_ref = all_casefolds();
use Unicode::UCD 'casespec';
my $casespec = casespec($codepoint);
use Unicode::UCD 'charblock';
my $charblock  = charblock($codepoint);
use Unicode::UCD 'charscript';
my $charscript = charscript($codepoint);
use Unicode::UCD 'charblocks';
my $charblocks = charblocks();
use Unicode::UCD 'charscripts';
my $charscripts = charscripts();
use Unicode::UCD qw(charscript charinrange);
my $range = charscript($script);
print "parece $script\n" if charinrange($range, $codepoint);
use Unicode::UCD qw(general_categories bidi_types);
my $categories = general_categories();
my $types = bidi_types();
use Unicode::UCD 'prop_aliases';
my @space_names = prop_aliases("space");
use Unicode::UCD 'prop_value_aliases';
my @gc_punct_names = prop_value_aliases("Gc", "Punct");
use Unicode::UCD 'prop_values';
my @all_EA_short_names = prop_values("East_Asian_Width");
use Unicode::UCD 'prop_invlist';
my @puncts = prop_invlist("gc=punctuation");
use Unicode::UCD 'prop_invmap';
my ($list_ref, $map_ref, $format, $missing)
                                  = prop_invmap("General Category");
use Unicode::UCD 'search_invlist';
my $index = search_invlist(\@invlist, $code_point);
# La siguiente función solo debe usarse internamente en
# implementaciones del Algoritmo de Normalización Unicode, y hay
# mejores opciones que esta.
use Unicode::UCD 'compexcl';
my $compexcl = compexcl($codepoint);
use Unicode::UCD 'namedseq';
my $namedseq = namedseq($named_sequence_name);
my $unicode_version = Unicode::UCD::UnicodeVersion();
my $convert_to_numeric =
          Unicode::UCD::num("\N{VAI DIGIT ONE}\N{VAI DIGIT TWO}");

DESCRIPCIÓN

El módulo Unicode::UCD ofrece una serie de funciones que proporcionan una interfaz sencilla a la Base de Datos de Caracteres Unicode.

argumento code point

Algunas de las funciones se llaman con un argumento de punto de código, que es un escalar decimal o hexadecimal que designa un punto de código en el conjunto de caracteres nativo de la plataforma (extendido a Unicode), o una cadena que contiene "U+" seguido de hexadecimales que designan un punto de código Unicode. Un 0 inicial forzará una interpretación hexadecimal, al igual que un dígito hexadecimal que no sea un dígito decimal.

Ejemplos:

223     # Decimal 223 en el conjunto de caracteres nativo
0223    # Hexadecimal 223, nativo (= 547 decimal)
0xDF    # Hexadecimal DF, nativo (= 223 decimal)
'0xDF'  # Forma de cadena de hexadecimal (= 223 decimal)
'U+DF'  # Hexadecimal DF, en el conjunto de caracteres de Unicode
                          (= LETRA LATINA MINÚSCULA S CON ACENTO
Circunflejo)

Tenga en cuenta que el punto de código más alto en Unicode es U+10FFFF.

charinfo()

use Unicode::UCD 'charinfo';
my $charinfo = charinfo(0x41);

Esto devuelve información sobre el argumento de "punto de código" de entrada como una referencia a un hash de campos según lo definido por el estándar Unicode. Si el "argumento de punto de código" no está asignado en el estándar (es decir, tiene la categoría general "Cn" que significa "Sin asignar") o es un no-carácter (lo que significa que está garantizado que nunca se asignará en el estándar), se devuelve "undef".

Los campos que no son aplicables al argumento de punto de código particular existen en el hash devuelto y están vacíos.

Para obtener resultados que sean menos "crudos" que los que devuelve esta función, o para obtener los valores de cualquier propiedad, no solo de las pocas cubiertas por esta función, use la función "charprop()".

Las claves en el hash con los significados de sus valores son:

code
el argumento nativo de entrada "punto de código" expresado en hexadecimal, con ceros iniciales añadidos si es necesario para que contenga al menos cuatro dígitos hexadecimales.
name
nombre de code, todo EN MAYÚSCULAS. Algunos puntos de código de tipo control no tienen nombres. Este campo estará vacío para los puntos de código "Surrogate" y "Private Use", y para los otros sin nombre, contendrá una descripción entre corchetes angulares, como "<control>".
category
El nombre corto de la categoría general de code. Esto coincidirá con una de las claves en el hash devuelto por "general_categories()".

La función "prop_value_aliases()" puede usarse para obtener todos los sinónimos del nombre de la categoría.

combining
el número de clase de combinación para code utilizado en el Algoritmo de Ordenamiento Canónico. Para Unicode 5.1, esto se describe en la Sección 3.11 "Comportamiento de Ordenamiento Canónico" disponible en http://www.unicode.org/versions/Unicode5.1.0/

La función "prop_value_aliases()" puede usarse para obtener todos los sinónimos del número de clase de combinación.

bidi
tipo bidireccional de code. Esto coincidirá con una de las claves en el hash devuelto por "bidi_types()".

La función "prop_value_aliases()" puede usarse para obtener todos los sinónimos del nombre del tipo bidi.

decomposition
está vacío si code no tiene descomposición; o son uno o más códigos (separados por espacios) que, tomados en orden, representan una descomposición para code. Cada uno tiene al menos cuatro dígitos hexadecimales. Los códigos pueden ir precedidos por una palabra entre corchetes angulares, luego un espacio, como "<compat> ", lo que indica el tipo de descomposición.

Esta descomposición puede ser una intermedia cuyos componentes también son descomponibles. Use Unicode::Normalize para obtener la descomposición final en un solo paso.

decimal
si code representa un dígito decimal, este es su valor numérico entero.
digit
si code representa algún otro número similar a un dígito, este es su valor numérico entero.
numeric
si code representa un número entero o racional, este es su valor numérico. Los valores racionales se expresan como una cadena como "1/4".
mirrored
"Y" o "N" que designa si code se refleja en texto bidireccional.
unicode10
nombre de code en el estándar Unicode 1.0 si existía para este punto de código y es diferente del nombre actual.
comment
A partir de Unicode 6.0, esto siempre está vacío.
upper
es, si no está vacío, el mapeo en mayúsculas para code expresado como al menos cuatro dígitos hexadecimales. Esto indica que el mapeo completo en mayúsculas es un solo carácter, y es idéntico al mapeo simple (solo de un solo carácter). Cuando este campo está vacío, significa que el mapeo simple en mayúsculas es code en sí; necesitará algún otro medio, (como "charprop()" o "casespec()" para obtener el mapeo completo.
lower
es, si no está vacío, el mapeo en minúsculas para code expresado como al menos cuatro dígitos hexadecimales. Esto indica que el mapeo completo en minúsculas es un solo carácter, y es idéntico al mapeo simple (solo de un solo carácter). Cuando este campo está vacío, significa que el mapeo simple en minúsculas es code en sí; necesitará algún otro medio, (como "charprop()" o

Este sitio no está asociado de ninguna forma con Arch Linux ni afiliado a este proyecto. El contenido es únicamente una traducción de las páginas del manual de Arch publicadas en man.archlinux.org.

The charinfo() function returns a reference to a hash containing information about the Unicode character specified by the argument. The argument can be a character code point (integer or string like "U+0041"), or a character itself.

The keys of the hash are the Unicode properties, and the values are the corresponding values for the character. You can use either the full property name (e.g. General_Category) or one of its synonyms (e.g. gc). Some properties have multiple synonyms; the hash will contain all of them as keys.

The following properties are available:

General_Category
or gc, etc. The general category of the character. This is a string like "Lu" (Letter, uppercase) or "Ll" (Letter, lowercase).
Uppercase_Mapping
or uc. If non-empty, this is the uppercase mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full uppercase mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple uppercase mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
Titlecase_Mapping
or title. is, if non-empty, the titlecase mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full titlecase mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple titlecase mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
Lowercase_Mapping
or lower. If non-empty, this is the lowercase mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full lowercase mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple lowercase mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
Decomposition_Mapping
or comp. If non-empty, this is the decomposition mapping for code expressed as a sequence of at least four hexdigits, separated by spaces. The default mapping is the character itself, but this field contains the mapping used for compatibility, canonical decomposition, etc. (See Unicode standard, section 3.11 and Annex 15.)

This field is empty for characters that don't have a decomposition mapping.

Numeric_Value
or numeric. If non-empty, this is the numeric value of the character, which may be an integer or a fraction (represented as a string like "1/3"). This field is empty for characters that don't have a numeric value.
Combining_Class
or ccc. The canonical combining class for the character. This is an integer between 0 and 240. This field is empty for characters that don't have a combining class (implicitly 0).
Bidi_Mirroring_Glyph
or bmg. The bidi mirroring glyph for the character, if any. This is a character code point.
Isomorphic_Mapping
or isomorphic. If non-empty, this is the isomorphic mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
General_Category_Mask
or gcm, etc. A bitmask representing the character's general category. This is useful for testing if a character belongs to a group of categories (e.g., all letters). The mask is composed of bits defined by the constants "General_Category_Mask_L", "General_Category_Mask_M", etc. See "General_Category_Mask_X".
Script
or sc. The script the character belongs to (e.g., "Latin", "Greek"). This is the older "Script" property value, and not the improved "Script_Extensions" value.
Script_Extensions
or scx. The script extensions for the character. This can be a comma-separated list of scripts if the character belongs to multiple scripts.
Block
or blk. The block the character belongs to (e.g., "Basic Latin", "Greek and Coptic").
East_Asian_Width
or ea. The East Asian width category of the character (e.g., "Na" (Narrow), "W" (Wide), "F" (Fullwidth), "H" (Halfwidth), "A" (Ambiguous), "N" (Neutral)).
Name
The official Unicode name of the character.

Note that you cannot do (de)composition and casing based solely on the decomposition, combining, lower, upper, and title fields; you will need also the "casespec()" function and the "Composition_Exclusion" property. (Or you could just use the lc(), uc(), and ucfirst() functions, and the Unicode::Normalize module.)

The values for Uppercase_Mapping, Titlecase_Mapping, Lowercase_Mapping, and Isomorphic_Mapping are only provided if they differ from the character itself. If they are empty, it means the mapping is the character itself. You will need to use the "casespec()" function to get the full mapping in those cases. The values are given in hexdigits, like "0041".

The value for Decomposition_Mapping is provided if it differs from the character itself. If it is empty, the character does not have a decomposition mapping (or it is the character itself). The mappings are given as a space-separated string of hexdigits.

The value for Numeric_Value is provided if the character has one. It can be an integer or a fractional string (e.g., "1/3").

The value for Combining_Class is provided if it is non-zero. A value of 0 is implicit for characters without a combining class.

The values for Script and Script_Extensions are the script names. Script_Extensions may contain multiple scripts separated by commas.

The value for Block is the block name.

The value for East_Asian_Width is a single character code representing the width category.

The value for Name is the official Unicode name of the character.

Uppercase_Mapping
or uc. If non-empty, this is the uppercase mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full uppercase mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple uppercase mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
Titlecase_Mapping
or title. is, if non-empty, the titlecase mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full titlecase mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple titlecase mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
Lowercase_Mapping
or lower. If non-empty, this is the lowercase mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full lowercase mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple lowercase mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
Decomposition_Mapping
or comp. If non-empty, this is the decomposition mapping for code expressed as a sequence of at least four hexdigits, separated by spaces. The default mapping is the character itself, but this field contains the mapping used for compatibility, canonical decomposition, etc. (See Unicode standard, section 3.11 and Annex 15.)

This field is empty for characters that don't have a decomposition mapping.

Numeric_Value
or numeric. If non-empty, this is the numeric value of the character, which may be an integer or a fraction (represented as a string like "1/3"). This field is empty for characters that don't have a numeric value.
Combining_Class
or ccc. The canonical combining class for the character. This is an integer between 0 and 240. This field is empty for characters that don't have a combining class (implicitly 0).
Bidi_Mirroring_Glyph
or bmg. The bidi mirroring glyph for the character, if any. This is a character code point.
Isomorphic_Mapping
or isomorphic. If non-empty, this is the isomorphic mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
General_Category_Mask
or gcm, etc. A bitmask representing the character's general category. This is useful for testing if a character belongs to a group of categories (e.g., all letters). The mask is composed of bits defined by the constants "General_Category_Mask_L", "General_Category_Mask_M", etc. See "General_Category_Mask_X".
Script
or sc. The script the character belongs to (e.g., "Latin", "Greek"). This is the older "Script" property value, and not the improved "Script_Extensions" value.
Script_Extensions
or scx. The script extensions for the character. This can be a comma-separated list of scripts if the character belongs to multiple scripts.
Block
or blk. The block the character belongs to (e.g., "Basic Latin", "Greek and Coptic").
East_Asian_Width
or ea. The East Asian width category of the character (e.g., "Na" (Narrow), "W" (Wide), "F" (Fullwidth), "H" (Halfwidth), "A" (Ambiguous), "N" (Neutral)).
Name
The official Unicode name of the character.

Note that you cannot do (de)composition and casing based solely on the decomposition, combining, lower, upper, and title fields; you will need also the "casespec()" function and the "Composition_Exclusion" property. (Or you could just use the lc(), uc(), and ucfirst() functions, and the Unicode::Normalize module.)

The values for Uppercase_Mapping, Titlecase_Mapping, Lowercase_Mapping, and Isomorphic_Mapping are only provided if they differ from the character itself. If they are empty, it means the mapping is the character itself. You will need to use the "casespec()" function to get the full mapping in those cases. The values are given in hexdigits, like "0041".

The value for Decomposition_Mapping is provided if it differs from the character itself. If it is empty, the character does not have a decomposition mapping (or it is the character itself). The mappings are given as a space-separated string of hexdigits.

The value for Numeric_Value is provided if the character has one. It can be an integer or a fractional string (e.g., "1/3").

The value for Combining_Class is provided if it is non-zero. A value of 0 is implicit for characters without a combining class.

The values for Script and Script_Extensions are the script names. Script_Extensions may contain multiple scripts separated by commas.

The value for Block is the block name.

The value for East_Asian_Width is a single character code representing the width category.

The value for Name is the official Unicode name of the character.

or isomorphic. If non-empty, this is the isomorphic mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
General_Category_Mask
or gcm, etc. A bitmask representing the character's general category. This is useful for testing if a character belongs to a group of categories (e.g., all letters). The mask is composed of bits defined by the constants "General_Category_Mask_L", "General_Category_Mask_M", etc. See "General_Category_Mask_X".
Script
or sc. The script the character belongs to (e.g., "Latin", "Greek"). This is the older "Script" property value, and not the improved "Script_Extensions" value.
Script_Extensions
or scx. The script extensions for the character. This can be a comma-separated list of scripts if the character belongs to multiple scripts.
Block
or blk. The block the character belongs to (e.g., "Basic Latin", "Greek and Coptic").
East_Asian_Width
or ea. The East Asian width category of the character (e.g., "Na" (Narrow), "W" (Wide), "F" (Fullwidth), "H" (Halfwidth), "A" (Ambiguous), "N" (Neutral)).
Name
The official Unicode name of the character.

Note that you cannot do (de)composition and casing based solely on the decomposition, combining, lower, upper, and title fields; you will need also the "casespec()" function and the "Composition_Exclusion" property. (Or you could just use the lc(), uc(), and ucfirst() functions, and the Unicode::Normalize module.)

The values for Uppercase_Mapping, Titlecase_Mapping, Lowercase_Mapping, and Isomorphic_Mapping are only provided if they differ from the character itself. If they are empty, it means the mapping is the character itself. You will need to use the "casespec()" function to get the full mapping in those cases. The values are given in hexdigits, like "0041".

The value for Decomposition_Mapping is provided if it differs from the character itself. If it is empty, the character does not have a decomposition mapping (or it is the character itself). The mappings are given as a space-separated string of hexdigits.

The value for Numeric_Value is provided if the character has one. It can be an integer or a fractional string (e.g., "1/3").

The value for Combining_Class is provided if it is non-zero. A value of 0 is implicit for characters without a combining class.

The values for Script and Script_Extensions are the script names. Script_Extensions may contain multiple scripts separated by commas.

The value for Block is the block name.

The value for East_Asian_Width is a single character code representing the width category.

The value for Name is the official Unicode name of the character.

Uppercase_Mapping
or uc. If non-empty, this is the uppercase mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full uppercase mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple uppercase mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
Titlecase_Mapping
or title. is, if non-empty, the titlecase mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full titlecase mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple titlecase mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
Lowercase_Mapping
or lower. If non-empty, this is the lowercase mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full lowercase mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple lowercase mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
Decomposition_Mapping
or comp. If non-empty, this is the decomposition mapping for code expressed as a sequence of at least four hexdigits, separated by spaces. The default mapping is the character itself, but this field contains the mapping used for compatibility, canonical decomposition, etc. (See Unicode standard, section 3.11 and Annex 15.)

This field is empty for characters that don't have a decomposition mapping.

Numeric_Value
or numeric. If non-empty, this is the numeric value of the character, which may be an integer or a fraction (represented as a string like "1/3"). This field is empty for characters that don't have a numeric value.
Combining_Class
or ccc. The canonical combining class for the character. This is an integer between 0 and 240. This field is empty for characters that don't have a combining class (implicitly 0).
Bidi_Mirroring_Glyph
or bmg. The bidi mirroring glyph for the character, if any. This is a character code point.
Isomorphic_Mapping
or isomorphic. If non-empty, this is the isomorphic mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.

or scx. The script extensions for the character. This can be a comma-separated list of scripts if the character belongs to multiple scripts.

or blk. The block the character belongs to (e.g., "Basic Latin", "Greek and Coptic").

or ea. The East Asian width category of the character (e.g., "Na" (Narrow), "W" (Wide), "F" (Fullwidth), "H" (Halfwidth), "A" (Ambiguous), "N" (Neutral)).
Name
The official Unicode name of the character.

Note that you cannot do (de)composition and casing based solely on the decomposition, combining, lower, upper, and title fields; you will need also the "casespec()" function and the "Composition_Exclusion" property. (Or you could just use the lc(), uc(), and ucfirst() functions, and the Unicode::Normalize module.)

The values for Uppercase_Mapping, Titlecase_Mapping, Lowercase_Mapping, and Isomorphic_Mapping are only provided if they differ from the character itself. If they are empty, it means the mapping is the character itself. You will need to use the "casespec()" function to get the full mapping in those cases. The values are given in hexdigits, like "0041".

The value for Decomposition_Mapping is provided if it differs from the character itself. If it is empty, the character does not have a decomposition mapping (or it is the character itself). The mappings are given as a space-separated string of hexdigits.

The value for Numeric_Value is provided if the character has one. It can be an integer or a fractional string (e.g., "1/3").

The value for Combining_Class is provided if it is non-zero. A value of 0 is implicit for characters without a combining class.

The values for Script and Script_Extensions are the script names. Script_Extensions may contain multiple scripts separated by commas.

The value for Block is the block name.

The value for East_Asian_Width is a single character code representing the width category.

The value for Name is the official Unicode name of the character.

Uppercase_Mapping
or uc. If non-empty, this is the uppercase mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full uppercase mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple uppercase mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
Titlecase_Mapping
or title. is, if non-empty, the titlecase mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full titlecase mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple titlecase mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
Lowercase_Mapping
or lower. If non-empty, this is the lowercase mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full lowercase mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple lowercase mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
Decomposition_Mapping
or comp. If non-empty, this is the decomposition mapping for code expressed as a sequence of at least four hexdigits, separated by spaces. The default mapping is the character itself, but this field contains the mapping used for compatibility, canonical decomposition, etc. (See Unicode standard, section 3.11 and Annex 15.)

This field is empty for characters that don't have a decomposition mapping.

Numeric_Value
or numeric. If non-empty, this is the numeric value of the character, which may be an integer or a fraction (represented as a string like "1/3"). This field is empty for characters that don't have a numeric value.
Combining_Class
or ccc. The canonical combining class for the character. This is an integer between 0 and 240. This field is empty for characters that don't have a combining class (implicitly 0).
Bidi_Mirroring_Glyph
or bmg. The bidi mirroring glyph for the character, if any. This is a character code point.
Isomorphic_Mapping
or isomorphic. If non-empty, this is the isomorphic mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
General_Category_Mask
or gcm, etc. A bitmask representing the character's general category. This is useful for testing if a character belongs to a group of categories (e.g., all letters). The mask is composed of bits defined by the constants "General_Category_Mask_L", "General_Category_Mask_M", etc. See "General_Category_Mask_X".
Script
or sc. The script the character belongs to (e.g., "Latin", "Greek"). This is the older "Script" property value, and not the improved "Script_Extensions" value.
Script_Extensions
or scx. The script extensions for the character. This can be a comma-separated list of scripts if the character belongs to multiple scripts.
Block
or blk. The block the character belongs to (e.g., "Basic Latin", "Greek and Coptic").
East_Asian_Width
or ea. The East Asian width category of the character (e.g., "Na" (Narrow), "W" (Wide), "F" (Fullwidth), "H" (Halfwidth), "A" (Ambiguous), "N" (Neutral)).
Name
The official Unicode name of the character.

The value for Numeric_Value is provided if the character has one. It can be an integer or a fractional string (e.g., "1/3").

The value for Combining_Class is provided if it is non-zero. A value of 0 is implicit for characters without a combining class.

The values for Script and Script_Extensions are the script names. Script_Extensions may contain multiple scripts separated by commas.

The value for Block is the block name.

The value for East_Asian_Width is a single character code representing the width category.

The value for Name is the official Unicode name of the character.

Uppercase_Mapping
or uc. If non-empty, this is the uppercase mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full uppercase mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple uppercase mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
Titlecase_Mapping
or title. is, if non-empty, the titlecase mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full titlecase mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple titlecase mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
Lowercase_Mapping
or lower. If non-empty, this is the lowercase mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full lowercase mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple lowercase mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
Decomposition_Mapping
or comp. If non-empty, this is the decomposition mapping for code expressed as a sequence of at least four hexdigits, separated by spaces. The default mapping is the character itself, but this field contains the mapping used for compatibility, canonical decomposition, etc. (See Unicode standard, section 3.11 and Annex 15.)

This field is empty for characters that don't have a decomposition mapping.

Numeric_Value
or numeric. If non-empty, this is the numeric value of the character, which may be an integer or a fraction (represented as a string like "1/3"). This field is empty for characters that don't have a numeric value.
Combining_Class
or ccc. The canonical combining class for the character. This is an integer between 0 and 240. This field is empty for characters that don't have a combining class (implicitly 0).
Bidi_Mirroring_Glyph
or bmg. The bidi mirroring glyph for the character, if any. This is a character code point.
Isomorphic_Mapping
or isomorphic. If non-empty, this is the isomorphic mapping for code expressed as at least four hexdigits. This indicates that the full mapping is a single character, and is identical to the simple (single-character only) mapping. When this field is empty, it means that the simple mapping is code itself; you'll need some other means, (like "charprop()" or "casespec()") to get the full mapping.
General_Category_Mask
or gcm, etc. A bitmask representing the character's general category. This is useful for testing if a character belongs to a group of categories (e.g., all letters). The mask is composed of bits defined by the constants "General_Category_Mask_L", "General_Category_Mask_M", etc. See "General_Category_Mask_X".
Script
or sc. The script the character belongs to (e.g., "Latin", "Greek"). This is the older "Script" property value, and not the improved "Script_Extensions" value.
Script_Extensions
or scx. The script extensions for the character. This can be a comma-separated list of scripts if the character belongs to multiple scripts.
Block
or blk. The block the character belongs to (e.g., "Basic Latin", "Greek and Coptic").
East_Asian_Width
or ea. The East Asian width category of the character (e.g., "Na" (Narrow), "W" (Wide), "F" (Fullwidth), "H" (Halfwidth), "A" (Ambiguous), "N" (Neutral)).
Name
The official Unicode name of the character.

Note that you cannot do (de)composition and casing based solely on the decomposition, combining, lower, upper, and title fields; you will need also the "casespec()" function and the "Composition_Exclusion" property. (Or you could just use the lc(), uc(), and ucfirst() functions, and the Unicode::Normalize module.)

The values for Uppercase_Mapping, Titlecase_Mapping, Lowercase_Mapping, and Isomorphic_Mapping are only provided if they differ from the character itself. charinrange()

In addition to using the "\p{Blk=...}" and "\P{Blk=...}" constructs, you can also test whether a code point is in the range as returned by "charblock()" and "charscript()" or as the values of the hash returned by "charblocks()" and "charscripts()" by using charinrange():

use Unicode::UCD qw(charscript charinrange);
$range = charscript('Hiragana');
print "looks like hiragana\n" if charinrange($range, $codepoint);

general_categories()

use Unicode::UCD 'general_categories';
my $categories = general_categories();

This returns a reference to a hash which has short general category names (such as "Lu", "Nd", "Zs", "S") as keys and long names (such as "UppercaseLetter", "DecimalNumber", "SpaceSeparator", "Symbol") as values. The hash is reversible in case you need to go from the long names to the short names. The general category is the one returned from "charinfo()" under the "category" key.

The "prop_values()" and "prop_value_aliases()" functions can be used as an alternative to this function; the first returning a simple list of the short category names; and the second gets all the synonyms of a given category name.

bidi_types()

use Unicode::UCD 'bidi_types';
my $categories = bidi_types();

This returns a reference to a hash which has the short bidi (bidirectional) type names (such as "L", "R") as keys and long names (such as "Left-to-Right", "Right-to-Left") as values. The hash is reversible in case you need to go from the long names to the short names. The bidi type is the one returned from "charinfo()" under the "bidi" key. For the exact meaning of the various bidi classes the Unicode TR9 is recommended reading: http://www.unicode.org/reports/tr9/ (as of Unicode 5.0.0)

The "prop_values()" and "prop_value_aliases()" functions can be used as an alternative to this function; the first returning a simple list of the short bidi type names; and the second gets all the synonyms of a given bidi type name.

compexcl()

WARNING: Unicode discourages the use of this function or any of the alternative mechanisms listed in this section (the documentation of compexcl()), except internally in implementations of the Unicode Normalization Algorithm. You should be using Unicode::Normalize directly instead of these. Using these will likely lead to half-baked results.

use Unicode::UCD 'compexcl';
my $compexcl = compexcl(0x09dc);

This routine returns "undef" if the Unicode version being used is so early that it doesn't have this property.

compexcl() is included for backwards compatibility, but as of Perl 5.12 and more modern Unicode versions, for most purposes it is probably more convenient to use one of the following instead:

my $compexcl = chr(0x09dc) =~ /\p{Comp_Ex};
my $compexcl = chr(0x09dc) =~ /\p{Full_Composition_Exclusion};

or even

my $compexcl = chr(0x09dc) =~ /\p{CE};
my $compexcl = chr(0x09dc) =~ /\p{Composition_Exclusion};

The first two forms return true if the "code point argument" should not be produced by composition normalization. For the final two forms to return true, it is additionally required that this fact not otherwise be determinable from the Unicode data base.

This routine behaves identically to the final two forms. That is, it does not return true if the code point has a decomposition consisting of another single code point, nor if its decomposition starts with a code point whose combining class is non-zero. Code points that meet either of these conditions should also not be produced by composition normalization, which is probably why you should use the "Full_Composition_Exclusion" property instead, as shown above.

The routine returns false otherwise.

casefold()

use Unicode::UCD 'casefold';
my $casefold = casefold(0xDF);
if (defined $casefold) {
    my @full_fold_hex = split / /, $casefold->{'full'};
    my $full_fold_string =
                join "", map {chr(hex($_))} @full_fold_hex;
    my @turkic_fold_hex =
                    split / /, ($casefold->{'turkic'} ne "")
                                    ? $casefold->{'turkic'}
                                    : $casefold->{'full'};
    my $turkic_fold_string =
                    join "", map {chr(hex($_))} @turkic_fold_hex;
}
if (defined $casefold && $casefold->{'simple'} ne "") {
    my $simple_fold_hex = $casefold->{'simple'};
    my $simple_fold_string = chr(hex($simple_fold_hex));
}

This returns the (almost) locale-independent case folding of the character specified by the "code point argument". (Starting in Perl v5.16, the core function fc() returns the "full" mapping (described below) faster than this does, and for entire strings.)

If there is no case folding for the input code point, "undef" is returned.

If there is a case folding for that code point, a reference to a hash with the following fields is returned:

code
the input native "code point argument" expressed in hexadecimal, with leading zeros added if necessary to make it contain at least four hexdigits
full
one or more codes (separated by spaces) that, taken in order, give the code points for the case folding for code. Each has at least four hexdigits.
simple
is empty, or is exactly one code with at least four hexdigits which can be used as an alternative case folding when the calling program cannot cope with the fold being a sequence of multiple code points. If full is just one code point, then simple equals full. If there is no single code point folding defined for code, then simple is the empty string. Otherwise, it is an inferior, but still better-than-nothing alternative folding to full.
mapping
is the same as simple if simple is not empty, and it is the same as full otherwise. It can be considered to be the simplest possible folding for code. It is defined primarily for backwards compatibility.
status
is "C" (for "common") if the best possible fold is a single code point (simple equals full equals mapping). It is "S" if there are distinct folds, simple and full (mapping equals simple). And it is "F" if there is only a full fold (mapping equals full; simple is empty). Note that this describes the contents of mapping. It is defined primarily for backwards compatibility.

For Unicode versions between 3.1 and 3.1.1 inclusive, status can also be "I" which is the same as "C" but is a special case for dotted uppercase I and dotless lowercase i:

* If you use this "I" mapping
the result is case-insensitive, but dotless and dotted I's are not distinguished
* If you exclude this "I" mapping
the result is not fully case-insensitive, but dotless and dotted I's are distinguished
turkic
contains any special folding for Turkic languages. For versions of Unicode starting with 3.2, this field is empty unless code has a different folding in Turkic languages, in which case it is one or more codes (separated by spaces) that, taken in order, give the code points for the case folding for code in those languages. Each code has at least four hexdigits. Note that this folding does not maintain canonical equivalence without additional processing.

For Unicode versions between 3.1 and 3.1.1 inclusive, this field is empty unless there is a special folding for Turkic languages, in which case status is "I", and mapping, full, simple, and turkic are all equal.

Programs that want complete generality and the best folding results should use the folding contained in the full field. But note that the fold for some code points will be a sequence of multiple code points.

Programs that can't cope with the fold mapping being multiple code points can use the folding contained in the simple field, with the loss of some generality. In Unicode 5.1, about 7% of the defined foldings have no single code point folding.

The mapping and status fields are provided for backwards compatibility for existing programs. They contain the same values as in previous versions of this function.

Locale is not completely independent. The turkic field contains results to use when the locale is a Turkic language.

For more information about case mappings see http://www.unicode.org/reports/tr21

all_casefolds()

use Unicode::UCD 'all_casefolds';
my $all_folds_ref = all_casefolds();
foreach my $char_with_casefold (sort { $a <=> $b }
                                keys %$all_folds_ref)
{
    printf "%04X:", $char_with_casefold;
    my $casefold_info = $all_folds_ref->{$char_with_casefold};
    if (defined $casefold_info) {
        print " full=", $casefold_info->{'full'};
        if ($casefold_info->{'simple'} ne $casefold_info->{'full'}) {
            print " simple=", $casefold_info->{'simple'};
        }
        if ($casefold_info->{'turkic'} ne "") {
            print " turkic=", $casefold_info->{'turkic'};
        }
        print " status=", $casefold_info->{'status'};
    }
    print "\n";
}

This function returns a reference to a hash which contains an entry for every Unicode character that has a case folding defined for it. The keys of the hash are the numeric code points of the characters, and the values are the same hash references as are returned by "casefold()".

Note that the keys of the hash are numbers, not strings.

charwidth()

use Unicode::UCD 'charwidth';
my $width = charwidth(0x41); # A
my $width = charwidth(0x00A1); # ¡
my $width = charwidth(0x1100); # Hangul Choseong Kiyeok
my $width = charwidth(0x20A1); # Euro sign
my $width = charwidth(0x2300); # APL Functional Symbol Quadrilateral

This function returns the number of columns the character represented by the "code point argument" will occupy on a standard terminal. This is a value between 0 and 2, inclusive. 0 means that the character does not display. 1 means that the character takes up one column. 2 means that the character takes up two columns.

The value of 0 is returned for characters that have the general category "Me" (e.g. combining marks). Also, characters that are not defined in the Unicode standard will return 0. The latter is true for characters with code points above 0x10FFFF.

The value of 2 is returned for characters that have the general category "Lo" (e.g. CJK ideographs) or East Asian Wide characters.

All other characters return 1.

This function is deprecated as of Perl 5.14; use the Text::UCD::charwidth() module instead.

charwidth_utf8()

use Unicode::UCD 'charwidth_utf8';
my $width = charwidth_utf8("A");
my $width = charwidth_utf8("¡");
my $width = charwidth_utf8("˜");

This function returns the number of columns the character represented by the first character of the "string argument" will occupy on a standard terminal. This is a value between 0 and 2, inclusive. 0 means that the character does not display. 1 means that the character takes up one column. 2 means that the character takes up two columns.

The value of 0 is returned for characters that have the general category "Me" (e.g. combining marks). Also, characters that are not defined in the Unicode standard will return 0. The latter is true for characters with code points above 0x10FFFF.

The value of 2 is returned for characters that have the general category "Lo" (e.g. CJK ideographs) or East Asian Wide characters.

All other characters return 1.

This function is deprecated as of Perl 5.14; use the Text::UCD::charwidth() module instead.

charwidth_iswide()

use Unicode::UCD 'charwidth_iswide';
if (charwidth_iswide(0x41)) { ... }

This function returns true if the character represented by the "code point argument" takes up two columns on a standard terminal. Otherwise, it returns false.

This function is deprecated as of Perl 5.14; use the Text::UCD::charwidth() module instead.

charwidth_iscategory()

use Unicode::UCD 'charwidth_iscategory';
if (charwidth_iscategory(0x41, 'Lu')) { ... }

This function returns true if the character represented by the "code point argument" belongs to the general category specified by the "string argument". Otherwise, it returns false.

This function is deprecated as of Perl 5.14; use the Text::UCD::charwidth() module instead.

charwidth_iscombining()

use Unicode::UCD 'charwidth_iscombining';
if (charwidth_iscombining(0x41)) { ... }

This function returns true if the character represented by the "code point argument" is a combining character (general category "Me"). Otherwise, it returns false.

This function is deprecated as of Perl 5.14; use the Text::UCD::charwidth() module instead.

prop_values()

use Unicode::UCD 'prop_values';
my $values = prop_values('General_Category');
my $values = prop_values('gc'); # also works

This function returns a reference to a hash which maps the canonical names of Unicode properties to their short names. For example:

    {
        'General_Category' => 'gc',
        'Script'           => 'sc',
        'Age'              => 'age',
        'Bidi_Class'       => 'bc',
        'Decimal_Digit'    => 'isDec',
        ...
    }

This function is useful for finding the short names of properties that you intend to use in "charinfo()" or in regular expressions.

prop_value_aliases()

use Unicode::UCD 'prop_value_aliases';
my $aliases = prop_value_aliases('General_Category');
my $aliases = prop_value_aliases('gc'); # also works

This function returns a reference to a hash which maps the canonical names of Unicode properties to a hash reference of their allowed values and their aliases. For example:

    my $gc_aliases = prop_value_aliases('General_Category');
    my $lu_aliases = $gc_aliases->{'Lu'}; # returns {'Uppercase_Letter' => 'Lu'}
    my $uppercase_letter_aliases = $gc_aliases->{'Uppercase_Letter'}; # returns {'Lu' => 'Uppercase_Letter'}
    my $lu_short_name = (keys %$lu_aliases)[0]; # $lu_short_name is 'Lu'
    my $lu_long_name = (values %$lu_aliases)[0]; # $lu_long_name is 'Uppercase_Letter'

This function is useful for finding the allowed values and aliases for properties that you intend to use in "charinfo()" or in regular expressions.

names()

use Unicode::UCD 'names';
my $names = names('Latins');

Returns a reference to a hash which contains script names as keys, and a reference to an array of all characters that belong to that script as values. The characters in the array are code points, not characters.

For example, $names->{'Latins'} would return a reference to an array of all characters that belong to the Latin script.

blocks()

use Unicode::UCD 'blocks';
my $blocks = blocks();

This function returns a reference to a hash where the keys are the names of the Unicode blocks (e.g., "Basic Latin", "Hiragana") and the values are references to arrays of code points belonging to that block. The code points are integers.

Example:

    my $blocks = blocks();
    my @hiragana_codepoints = @{$blocks->{'Hiragana'}};
    foreach my $codepoint (@hiragana_codepoints) {
        print chr($codepoint);
    }

blocks_by_range()

use Unicode::UCD 'blocks_by_range';
my $blocks_by_range = blocks_by_range();

This function returns a reference to a hash where the keys are the names of the Unicode blocks (e.g., "Basic Latin", "Hiragana") and the values are references to arrays of two integers, representing the start and end code points of the block range.

Example:

    my $blocks_by_range = blocks_by_range();
    my $hiragana_range = $blocks_by_range->{'Hiragana'};
    my $hiragana_start = $hiragana_range->[0];
    my $hiragana_end = $hiragana_range->[1];
    print "Hiragana range: $hiragana_start - $hiragana_end\n";

scripts()

use Unicode::UCD 'scripts';
my $scripts = scripts();

This function returns a reference to a hash where the keys are the names of the Unicode scripts (e.g., "Latin", "Hiragana") and the values are references to arrays of code points belonging to that script. The code points are integers.

Example:

    my $scripts = scripts();
    my @hiragana_codepoints = @{$scripts->{'Hiragana'}};
    foreach my $codepoint (@hiragana_codepoints) {
        print chr($codepoint);
    }

scripts_by_range()

use Unicode::UCD 'scripts_by_range';
my $scripts_by_range = scripts_by_range();

This function returns a reference to a hash where the keys are the names of the Unicode scripts (e.g., "Latin", "Hiragana") and the values are references to arrays of two integers, representing the start and end code points of the script range.

Example:

    my $scripts_by_range = scripts_by_range();
    my $hiragana_range = $scripts_by_range->{'Hiragana'};
    my $hiragana_start = $hiragana_range->[0];
    my $hiragana_end = $hiragana_range->[1];
    print "Hiragana range: $hiragana_start - $hiragana_end\n";

script_extensions()

use Unicode::UCD 'script_extensions';
my $script_extensions = script_extensions();

This function returns a reference to a hash where the keys are Unicode code points (as integers) and the values are references to arrays of script names (as strings) that the code point belongs to. This includes scripts defined in the Script_Extensions property.

Example:

    my $script_extensions = script_extensions();
    my $codepoint = 0x3041; # Hiragana Letter Small A
    my @scripts = @{$script_extensions->{$codepoint}};
    print "Codepoint $codepoint belongs to scripts: @scripts\n";

script_extensions_by_range()

use Unicode::UCD 'script_extensions_by_range';
my $script_extensions_by_range = script_extensions_by_range();

This function returns a reference to a hash where the keys are Unicode script names (as strings) and the values are references to arrays of two integers, representing the start and end code points of the range for that script, including ranges from the Script_Extensions property.

Example:

    my $script_extensions_by_range = script_extensions_by_range();
    my $latin_ranges = $script_extensions_by_range->{'Latin'};
    foreach my $range (@$latin_ranges) {
        my $start = $range->[0];
        my $end = $range->[1];
        print "Latin script range: $start - $end\n";
    }

all_properties()

use Unicode::UCD 'all_properties';
my @properties = all_properties();

This function returns a list of all Unicode property names (short names) that are available in the current Unicode version.

all_property_values()

use Unicode::UCD 'all_property_values';
my $values = all_property_values('General_Category');

This function returns a reference to a hash where the keys are the canonical names of Unicode properties and the values are references to lists of all possible values for that property. For example:

    my $gc_values = all_property_values('General_Category');
    my @gc_list = @{$gc_values->{'General_Category'}}; # ('C', 'Cc', 'Cf', 'Co', 'Cs', 'L', 'Lc', 'Ll', 'Lm', 'Lo', 'Lt', 'Lu', 'M', 'Mc', 'Me', 'Mn', 'N', 'Nd', 'Nl', 'No', 'P', 'Pc', 'Pd', 'Pe', 'Pf', 'Pi', 'Po', 'Ps', 'S', 'Sc', 'Sk', 'Sm', 'So', 'Z', 'Zl', 'Zp', 'Zs')

property()

use Unicode::UCD 'property';
my $property_value = property(0x0041, 'General_Category');
print "The general category of 'A' is $property_value\n";

This function returns the value of the specified Unicode property for the given code point. The property name can be either the canonical name or the short name.

If the property is not defined for the given code point, it returns "undef".

properties()

use Unicode::UCD 'properties';
my $properties_ref = properties(0x0041);

This function returns a reference to a hash where the keys are the property names (short names) and the values are the corresponding property values for the given code point.

Example:

    my $properties_ref = properties(0x0041);
    print "General Category: ", $properties_ref->{'gc'}, "\n";
    print "Script: ", $properties_ref->{'sc'}, "\n";

use Unicode::UCD 'named';
my $name = named(0x0041);

use Unicode::UCD 'name_alias';

use Unicode::UCD 'name_aliases';

use Unicode::UCD 'category';

Returns "undef" if the code point is undefined.

use Unicode::UCD 'category_name';

script()

use Unicode::UCD 'script';
my $script = script(0x0041); # 'Latin'
my $script = script(0x00E9); # 'Latin'
my $script = script(0x0401); # 'Cyrillic'
my $script = script(0x3041); # 'Hiragana'

Returns the script name of the character specified by the "code point argument" (e.g., "Latin", "Cyrillic", "Hiragana").

Returns "Common" for characters that belong to the "Common" script (e.g., symbols, punctuation).

Returns "Unknown" if the code point is undefined.

script_of_codepoint()

use Unicode::UCD 'script_of_codepoint';
my $script = script_of_codepoint(0x0041); # 'Latn'
my $script = script_of_codepoint(0x0401); # 'Cyrl'

Returns the script code of the character specified by the "code point argument" (e.g., "Latn", "Cyrl", "Hira").

Returns "Zyyy" for characters that belong to the "Common" script (e.g., symbols, punctuation).

Returns "Zzzz" if the code point is undefined.

east_asian_width()

use Unicode::UCD 'east_asian_width';
my $width = east_asian_width(0x41); # 'Na' (Narrow)
my $width = east_asian_width(0x3041); # 'W' (Wide)
my $width = east_asian_width(0x1100); # 'W' (Wide)
my $width = east_asian_width(0x20A1); # 'Na' (Narrow)
my $width = east_asian_width(0x2300); # 'Na' (Narrow)

This function returns a single character string indicating the East Asian Width property of the character specified by the "code point argument".

The possible return values are:

'N'
Neutral: Includes control characters, non-spacing marks, etc.
'Na'
Narrow: Characters that occupy a single column (most characters).
'W'
Wide: Characters that occupy two columns (e.g., CJK ideographs).
'F'
Fullwidth: Characters that normally have a two-column width but are represented by a single code point (e.g., fullwidth Latin characters).
'H'
Halfwidth: Characters that normally have a single-column width but are represented by a double-width code point (e.g., halfwidth Katakana).
'A'
Ambiguous: Characters whose width depends on context (e.g., certain Greek and Cyrillic characters). This module treats them as Narrow.

Returns "undef" if the code point is undefined.

combining_class()

use Unicode::UCD 'combining_class';
my $class = combining_class(0x0041); # 0
my $class = combining_class(0x0301); # 001 (COMBINING ACUTE ACCENT)
my $class = combining_class(0x09dc); # 009 (BENGALI Vowel Sign Indic)

Returns the canonical combining class of the character specified by the "code point argument" as an integer. A value of 0 indicates that the character does not have a canonical combining class (or it is non-spacing).

Returns "undef" if the code point is undefined.

decomposition()

use Unicode::UCD 'decomposition';
my $decomp = decomposition(0x00E9); # hex: 0065 0301, canonical: é
my $decomp = decomposition(0x00C6); # hex: 0061 006C, canonical: æ

Returns the canonical decomposition of the character specified by the "code point argument". The decomposition is returned as a hash reference with the following keys:

hex
A space-separated string of hexadecimal code points that form the decomposition.
canonical
A string containing the characters represented by the decomposed code points.
type
The type of decomposition:
<
Canonical mapping (e.g., U+00E9 LATIN SMALL LETTER E WITH ACUTE is mapped to U+0065 LATIN SMALL LETTER E followed by U+0301 COMBINING ACUTE ACCENT).
=
Compatibility mapping (e.g., U+FB03 LATIN SMALL LIGATURE OSSZ is mapped to U+006F LATIN SMALL LETTER O followed by U+0073 LATIN SMALL LETTER S followed by U+0073 LATIN SMALL LETTER S).

Returns "undef" if the code point is undefined or does not have a decomposition.

decomposed_char()

use Unicode::UCD 'decomposed_char';
my $string = decomposed_char(0x00E9); # returns "e\x{301}"
my $string = decomposed_char(0x00C6); # returns "al"

Returns the decomposed form of the character specified by the "code point argument" as a string. This is equivalent to chr(hex($decomp->{'hex'})) where $decomp is the return value of "decomposition()".

Returns "undef" if the code point is undefined or does not have a decomposition.

normalized()

use Unicode::UCD 'normalized';
my $normalized_string = normalized("ér", FC); # NFD
my $normalized_string = normalized("ér", NFKC); # NFKC

This function is a wrapper around Unicode::Normalize. It returns the normalized form of the given string according to the specified normalization form.

The normalization form can be one of the following constants (defined in Unicode::Normalize):

NFC
NFD
NFKC
NFKD

Example:

    use Unicode::UCD 'normalized';
    use Unicode::Normalize qw(NFC NFD NFKC NFKD);
    my $string = "ér";
    print normalized($string, NFC), "\n"; # é r combined
    print normalized($string, NFD), "\n"; # e r combining acute accent
    print normalized($string, NFKC), "\n"; # é r combined
    print normalized($string, NFKD), "\n"; # e r combining acute accent

Note: This function is deprecated as of Perl 5.14. Use Unicode::Normalize directly.

compose()

use Unicode::UCD 'compose';
my $composed_codepoint = compose(0x0065, 0x0301); # returns 0xE9

Tries to compose the given sequence of code points into a single code point. Returns the composed code point on success, or "undef" if the sequence cannot be composed into a single code point.

Note: This function is deprecated as of Perl 5.14. Use Unicode::Normalize directly.

composite()

use Unicode::UCD 'composite';
my $composite_string = composite(0x00E9); # returns "e\x{301}"

Returns the composite (decomposed) form of the character specified by the "code point argument". This is equivalent to decomposed_char().

Note: This function is deprecated as of Perl 5.14. Use Unicode::Normalize directly.
